Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. Hi, Doctor. It's really nice to meet you. Um, thank you for seeing me. I... well, I hope I'm giving you the right information, I just want to make sure I'm doing this right. 

I guess what really scared me enough to actually come in today is... well, it's a little embarrassing to admit, to be honest. I've been, um, hearing my name called. Just my name, nothing bad or anything! But I'll hear it coming from the hallway at home, and... and it happened once at the warehouse where I work, too, when absolutely nobody was around. 

It's just strange because it sounds as real as my husband standing right next to me. I know that probably sounds a little crazy, I'm sorry. Is that the kind of thing I should be telling you?

Um... no, I haven't. I mean, I think this is sort of like what you were asking just a minute ago, right? About believing things that aren't true? I still... I really just don't have anything like that.

Well... I suppose... I mean, there was one time, but it's really nothing to worry about. It's just a silly thing, really. 

I was doing my crafts, you know, just making things at home, and... well, I got it in my head that I could turn it into a real retirement business. Like, right away. I just felt so sure that everyone would see I had figured out the perfect plan for our family, to take care of us. I thought it was going to be this huge success and I wouldn't have to stock shelves anymore. 

Of course, it didn't really pan out that way, and I realize now it was a bit... ambitious. I feel a little embarrassed even bringing it up, to be honest. It's just a silly daydream that got a bit out of hand, I suppose. 

Does that... does that answer your question better? I hope that's the kind of thing you were looking for. I really do want to be helpful.

Oh, um... well... I really didn't know who it was, or why. And I... I feel a bit foolish even admitting this, because I really don't want you to think badly of me, or think I'm making things up. But... well, to be completely honest, it's actually what scared me enough to finally come in and see you. 

I just... I would be at home, and I'd hear my name being called right from the hallway. And then it happened once at the warehouse, too, when absolutely nobody was in that section with me. I looked all over, but no one was there. But it sounded so clear... just exactly as real as if my husband was standing right next to me speaking to me. 

I didn't have any grand idea about why it was happening, and I just kept trying to ignore it so I wouldn't be a bother to my family. I'm so sorry, doctor, I know that probably sounds terribly strange. I hope you don't think I'm losing my mind... is it alright that I told you that? I just want to make sure I'm answering your questions the right way.

No, I haven't. I mean, at the warehouse, we all pretty much see and do the same things, you know? I don't ever feel like there's some secret thing going on that only I can tell is happening. I'm just... I'm just a normal team player, really. \n\nI feel a little bad that I keep saying no to all these questions, though. I really hope I'm being helpful to you. Is it alright that I don't have anything like that to report? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information so we can get everything sorted out. Am I doing okay?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a belief that something was true, even when others told you it wasn’t, or when there was no proof to support it?\n\nPatient: No, I... I really don't think I've ever had anything like that happen. I try to be pretty level-headed, you know? If my husband or the folks at work tell me I've got something wrong, I usually just listen to them. I don't like to argue or make a fuss. \n\nI really try to see things the way everyone else does. I don't want to be difficult or... or have people think I'm stubborn about things that aren't true. \n\nI'm sorry, I feel like I'm just giving you a bunch of \"no\"s today. I really am trying to answer everything as honestly as I can. Is it okay that I don't have any examples of that? I hope I'm still giving you what you need.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a strong feeling or belief about something that you know is probably not true, even if you’ve never told anyone about it?\n\nPatient: Um, no, doctor. I wouldn't want to get caught up in... in things that aren't true, you know? \n\nI really try to keep my feet on the ground. I wouldn't want anyone to think I was... well, that I wasn't thinking straight. \n\nI'm so sorry, I feel terrible that I keep giving you the same answer. I just want to make sure I'm doing this right. I'm not messing up your evaluation by not having these things happen, am I? I just want to be a good patient for you.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a belief that something was true, even when there was no proof to support it or when others showed you that it wasn’t true?\n\nPatient: Um... no, I haven't. I mean, I think this is sort of like what you were asking just a minute ago, right? About believing things that aren't true? I still... I really just don't have anything like that. \n\nI try really hard to just be agreeable and go with the flow. If my husband or my boss says something is a certain way, I just trust them. It's easier that way, and it keeps everyone happy. I wouldn't want to be the kind of person who insists on something when everyone else says it's wrong. \n\nI'm really, really sorry that I don't have a better answer for you. I feel like a broken record, just sitting here saying no over and over. Are you sure I'm not ruining your notes? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the kind of answers you need to do your job. Am I still being helpful?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something strongly that others might not agree with, even if you didn’t talk about it or act on it?\n\nPatient: I... well, I know we keep circling back to this, and I really wish I could give you a different answer so you could check off whatever box you need. Oh, dear... I'm so, so sorry. I feel like a broken record, and I know I must be testing your patience by now. I really am trying to understand what you're looking for, I promise I am.

I wouldn't want to be the kind of person who insists on something when everyone else says it's wrong. \n\nI'm really, really sorry that I don't have a better answer for you.
